mod/src/debug/kotlin/io/github/noxaur/dsmod/debug/capture/CutFixture.kt: /** * Cuts the events of one window out of [capture] into [fixture], redacted. [types] are the event types to keep, * and `container` keeps `slot` too. The tab list and the sidebar that tell [area], and each change of world, are kept * whatever [types] says, because a fixture without them replays as if the player never left. The fixture begins with the state at the * window's start: the last tab list and sidebar before it and the open container. A return to the player's inventory * that keeps its slots is written with every slot it kept, so a fixture never needs what came before it. A tab list * inside the window is kept only when one of the [info] rows or the area's row changed, and every one is kept when * `tablist` is a type and there are no [info] rows. A sidebar inside the window is kept when `sidebar` is a type, and * otherwise only when its place changed. A system line is kept when it matches [system], or always without one. * With [entities], the feature's filters, the entity events are kept as the feature hears them, starting with each * entity it reads that the client held at the window's start, so the fixture holds only the entities it reads. * Returns the number of lines written and of players renamed. */

mod/src/main/kotlin/io/github/noxaur/dsmod/core/feature/Feature.kt: /** * The part a feature holds, which `Game` runs. A feature turns events into its own state in [on], and turns that state * into what the player sees in the [hud], [slots] and [marks] it passes here, says something once with [notice] and * [alert], or keeps a server line out of chat with [hide]. The shared layers draw and print all of it, so a feature touches no screen itself. [switch] is * its switch in the settings, and while that is off, none of these run. * Outside [area] the feature shows nothing and sees only the tab list, the sidebar and a change of world. * * A feature declares itself once, here. Its id gives its title, which heads its HUD block and names its switch, and * [description] is what the switch's row says. Its other settings are declared as rows, as `val shortest = feature.number(...)`, * each saved under its key in the feature's [store] of the config file, so a feature file is the one place a setting is * named, described and given its default. `SettingsTree` places each feature once, which is also what lists it: a * feature placed nowhere never runs, and `FeaturesListedTest` fails on it. Two features that share behaviour each hold * a part for it, as Boss Beacon and Boss Tracer hold `BossMarks`, never a base class. * * Start from the feature closest to yours: * - Follow lines in chat: `DragonTimer`. * - Read the tab list's info rows, or the sidebar's the same way: `ProtectorStatus`. * - Read items and mark slots: `ItemRarity`. * - Show items on the HUD, from the player's own inventory or an entity near them: `EquipmentDisplay`. * - Change how the game draws, reading nothing from the server: `Fullbright`. * - Count something and keep the total: `SlayerTracker`. * - Hide a line the server sends: `HideCooldownMessages`. * - Shorten or hide text the server floats in the world, such as damage numbers: `DamageNumbers`. * - Tell the player something once, and read a setting besides the switch: `RareDropAlert`. * - Track a rate, and follow the player into and out of its area: `ZealotTracker`. * - Mark a place in the world, and read settings of a few kinds: `ProtectorSpawnBeam`. * - Read the entities the client holds, and mark them in the world: `BossBeacon`, with its part `BossMarks`. * - Act on a key the player binds, or change the camera: `FreeLook` and `Camera`, with `Cameras` in `edge` and the *   rules in `core/camera`. * - Handle the player's own input on a vanilla screen, reading nothing from the server: `CopyChat`, with `CopyChats` *   in `edge`. * - Settings the edge reads each frame, and a button that resets them: `Animations`, with `HeldItems` in `edge`. */
